We can design algorithms:
1. Start with parent height_sequence_1 length 192; evaluate.
2. Run iterative LP as parent but improved:
   - Normalize sum=1.
   - At each f, compute c = conv(f,f), M=max c.
   - Solve LP: max sum g s.t. A_g? A = Toeplitz-like where A_{k,i}=f_{k-i} if in range. Actually A*g = f*g: row k length n. Use scipy.sparse? Dense A maybe. Use HiGHS.
   - Rescale g to sum=1. Evaluate.
   - Step: f_new = (1-t)f + t g. Try line search over t grid, maybe use best. Or simply f=g? The LP g may have same or lower peak. For nonincreasing, use t=1 if improves else backtracking.
   - If LP fails, use analytical subgradient steps.

def lp_step(f, n, time_left, M_scale=1.0):
    c = np.convolve(f,f)
    M = c.max()
    # Build A such that A @ g <= M
    # If M <=0 return f
    # Solve max sum g
    r = linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*(1+1e-9)+1e-12, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve':True,'time_limit':T,'dual_feasibility_tolerance':...})
    g = ...
    if sumg>0: g/=sumg
    return g, M, c
```

Line search and update:
```
def improve_step(f, g, loc):
   best = (loc, f)
   for t in (1.0,0.9,0.8,0.5,0.3,0.2,...):
       cand = (1-t)f + t g
       s=sc(cand)
       if s<best: update
   # Also try g - alpha(f-g)? since g may be better alone, negative alpha may go past g.
   for alpha in (0.1,0.25,0.5): cand = g + alpha*(g-f); if nonneg: cand/=sum; score.
   return best
```

Stopping: If no improvement in e.g. 20 iterations, perturb:
- multiplicative noise: f * exp(σ normal) normalize; σ decreasing from 0.1 to 0.02.
- add random sparse bump, smooth.
- cross with another random candidate.
Then continue max iterations.

LP solver may converge to same g as f (if f stationary), then M same. If g≈f (cosine high) with no improvement, stop.

Constants and functions:
```
import numpy as np, time, math
try: from scipy.optimize import linprog
except: linprog=None
try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
except: minimize=None
TARGET=1.50274349
```

Fast score:
```
def _sc(a, n=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15: return inf
    # To mimic scaling invariance, maybe normalize to sum=1? score invariant if s scaled; use raw:
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    return 2.0*a.size*float(c.max())/(s*s)
```
Need cap 1000? For internal, if a>1000, evaluator clips to 1000. We maintain within. Use `_clean` for returning.

Normalize sum 1:
```
def _norm(a):
    a=np.clip(np.nan_to_num(a),0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return None
    return a/s
```

Best update:
```
def _upd(a,best):
   a=_norm(a)
   if None return
   # ensure finite, cap 1000 after normalization (should be small)
   s=_sc(a)
   if finite and s<best[0]: best[0]=s; best[1]=a.copy()
```

LP solve:
```
def _lp_matrix(f, fast=False):
    n=f.size
    if n <= 512:
       A=np.empty((2*n-1,n),float)
       for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=f
    else:
       # use indices maybe memory; same loop for all
       A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n),float)
       for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=f
    return A
```
Could do normalization by M inside:
```
def _lp_dir(f, maxtime, verbose=False):
   c=np.convolve(f,f); M=c.max()
   if M<=0: return None
   A=M? build A / M: We can build A via f/M to avoid scaling after.
   fM=f/M
   A[...] = fM
   b=np.ones(2*n-1)
   r=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=b+1e-12, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve':True, 'time_limit':maxtime, 'mip_rel_gap':...})
```
If M very small, f/M may large but okay. Use fM = f / M. Since f sum1, M maybe <1e-3; f/M up to maybe 1000? For n=192, constant M=0.0052, fM=1. If f peaked, f_i up to ~0.1, M~0.01? f/M~10. Good.

Time limit per LP: allocate `_lp_refine` with a max number of seconds. Each LP call should have a time limit e.g. `min(3.0, remaining_per_iter)`. But too many LP calls with small time limit may waste presolve. For n=192, no time limit or 2s ok. Use global deadline.

However, if we call LP with `time_limit` and it returns nonoptimal but x, we can use. `linprog` option names: For HiGHS in scipy: `options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': 10.0}`. Good.

How to check time inside LP? Can't interrupt; time_limit handles. Set presolve. For small n, presolve maybe overhead. Could set `presolve=False`? Presolve usually speeds. For n=192, presolve fine.

Matrix building using f/M: If M is max of c. For f normalized sum1, M≥? average 1/(2n); M maybe ~0.004. f/M entries O(1/n? no). Fine.

Line search after LP:
```
def _try_blend(f,g,loc):
    t_best=0; best_score=loc
    # g normalized sum1
    for t in (1.0, 0.9, 0.75, 0.6, 0.5, 0.4, 0.3, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05):
       cand = f + t*(g-f)
       sc=_sc(cand)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; t_best=t
    # extrapolate if g alone good
    if t_best==1.0:
       for alpha in (0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0): cand = g + alpha*(g-f); if min>= -1e-12: cand=clip; norm; sc; update
    return cand if improved else None
```
But computing many scores; can first check g score; if not better, maybe t=0.5. Use cheap evaluate via convolve.

LP_refine loop:
```
def _lp_refine(a0, stop_time, best, rng, max_iter=5000, noise_base=0.05):
    a=_norm(a0); loc=_sc(a); _upd(a,best)
    stag=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 1.0:
       it+=1
       g=_lp_dir(a, time_limit=...)
       if g is None:
          # use subgradient step?
          if stag>3: break
          stag+=1; continue
       g=_norm(g)
       if g is None: ...
       cand,sc=_blend(a,g,loc)
       if cand is not None:
          a=cand; loc=sc; _upd(a,best); stag=0
       else:
          stag+=1
          # perturb in noise scaled by stag
          if stag==1 or stag%5==0:
             # multiplicative
             sig = min(0.1, 0.02 + 0.001*stag)
             pa = _norm(a * np.exp(sig*rng.standard_normal(n)))
             psa=_sc(pa)

def _starts(n, best_arr, rng, count=20):
   starts=[]
   def add(a):
      a=_norm(a)

   # parent interpolation
   if best_arr is not None and best_arr.size>=2:

      add(np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), tp, best_arr))
   # constant
   add(np.ones(n))
   # exponentials
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   for c in (-3,-2,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,2,3): add(np.exp(c*(x-0.5)))

      for ph in 0, pi/2, pi:
         base=1+0.8*np.sin(2πkx+ph); add(clip(base,0,None)); add(1+0.5*...)

def _make_lse_obj(a_template, beta):
    n=a_template.size
    def obj(x, g=True):
       x=np.clip(x, -30,30)
       ex=np.exp(x); a=ex/ex.sum()
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
       cm=c.max()
       vals=beta*(c-cm)
       # vals <=0
       # stable logsumexp:
       m=np.max(vals)  # <=0
       z=np.exp(vals-m); zsum=z.sum()
       val=cm + (m+math.log(zsum))/beta
       if not g: return val
       w=z/zsum
       # grad_a_j = 2 sum_k w_k a_{k-j}
       # compute weighted via loops
       grad_a = np.zeros(n)
       # For each k with w_k>tol: for j=0..n-1: idx=k-j; if valid grad_a[j]+=2*w_k*a[idx]
       # O(n^2). For n=288 maybe okay; L-BFGS many calls? Could vectorize with matrix W.
       # Build W? For each eval, we can compute grad via convolution? Let's derive fast.
```

def optimize_one(a0, stop, best, rng, lp_time_limit=2.0, max_stag=80):
    a=norm(a0); loc=sc(a); upd
    n=a.size; stag=0; it=0
    while now<stop-2:
       it++
       # maybe if loc close, smaller sigma
       # Generate LP direction
       g = lp_dir(a, time_limit=min(lp_time_limit, stop-now-2))
       improved=False
       if g is not None:
           g=norm(g)
           # line search
           cand = choose_t(f,g,loc)
           if cand: update; improved=True
       if not improved:
           # try active amplitude local move? occasionally
           if stag%3==0:
               cand = local_active(a, loc, rng)
               if cand: update; improved=True
       if not improved:
           stag +=1
           # noise update
           sig = base/(1+stag*0.1) maybe
           if rng.random()<0.5: multiplicative noise
           else: add smooth noise

           if not accept and stag>... break
       if stag>max_stag: break
```

```
def _gen_starts(n, par, rng, limit=30):
    starts=[]; seen scores? Use dict? Just list and sort.
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    def add(arr):
       arr=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
       if arr.size!=n: return
       arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0, posinf=0, neginf=0)
       arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
       s=arr.sum()
       if s <=1e-12: return
       arr=arr/s
       # maybe eliminate duplicate? skip
       starts.append(arr)
    if par is not None and par.size>=2:
       add(np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,1,par.size), par))
       # smoothed parent: small gaussian? use np.convolve with [1,2,1]/4
    add(np.ones(n))
    for c in (-3,-2,-1.5,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,1.5,2,3): add(np.exp(c*(x-0.5)))
    for p in (0.2,0.5,1,2,3,5):
       add(x**p); add((1-x)**p); add((x*(1-x))**p); add(((x-0.25)**2+0.05)**-p) etc need positive.
    for k in range(1,6):
       for amp in (0.3,0.6,0.9):
           for ph in (0, math.pi/2, math.pi): add(1+amp*np.sin(2πk x+ph))
    for center in (0,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9,1):
       for width in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4): add(np.exp(-0.5*((x-center)/width)**2))
       for width... add(np.maximum(0, 1 - abs(x-center)/width))
    # random smooth: choose m points, values positive
    for _ in range(12):
       m=int(rng.integers(3, max(4, n//8)))
       px=np.sort(rng.random(m)); vals=rng.lognormal(0,1,m)
       add(np.interp(x, px, vals, right=vals[-1], left=vals[0]))
    for alpha in (0.1,0.3,0.7,1.5,3):
       add(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    # random binary then smooth: pick mask
    for _ in range(4):
       mask = rng.random(n) < 0.5
       m = mask.astype(float); if sum>0: add(predict smooth)
    # parent noise
    if par: add(interpolated * exp(0.2 normal)); add(interpolated * (1+0.2 sin))?
    # score sort
    scored=[( _sc(arr), arr) for arr in starts]
    scored=[... if finite]
    scored.sort(key=lambda t:t[0])
    return [arr for _,arr in scored[:limit]]
```

def _gen_starts(n, par, best_arr, rng, limit=12):
   if n<2: n=2
   starts=[]
   seen=[]
   def add(a):
       a=_norm(a)
       if a is None: return
       # dedup norm distance <1e-3 from seen? Use cheap
       for s in seen:
           if np.linalg.norm(a-s)<0.02:
               return
       seen.append(a)
       starts.append(a)
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   if par is not None and par.size>=2:
       add(np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,1,par.size), par))
       # smoothed parent
       k=(1,2,1); add(np.convolve(par_arr resized?)?)
   if best_arr is not None and best_arr.size>=2:
       add(np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,1,best_arr.size), best_arr))
   add(np.ones(n))
   ... etc
   scored=[]
   for a in starts:
       s=_sc(a)
       if math.isfinite(s): scored.append((s,a))
   scored.sort(key=lambda t:t[0])
   return [a for _,a in scored[:limit]]
```

Now `_lp_dir` needs to handle linprog. Let's code robust:
```
def _lp_dir(f, time_limit=3.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    n=f.size
    c=np.convolve(f,f)
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(M) or M<=0: return None
    fM=f/M
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=fM
    b=np.ones(2*n-1, dtype=np.float64) + 1e-11  # maybe
    c_obj=-np.ones(n)
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
    opts={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': max(0.5, min(10.0, time_limit))}
    # call with smaller time? 
    try:
       r=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=opts)
    except Exception:
       return None
    x=None
    if hasattr(r,'x') and r.x is not None:
       x=np.asarray(r.x,float).ravel()
    if x is None or x.size!=n: return None
    x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    x=np.clip(x,0,None)
    s=float(x.sum())
    if s<=1e-15: return None
    # optional feasibility check? If constraints too violated, maybe still use
    return x/s
```
Could `b` with 1e-11 enough? Since f feasible with Bf=c/M, active =1. Floating errors maybe 1+1e-13; add 1e-9: b=1+1e-9. Slight infeasibility allows g a bit beyond; direction still valid? It might produce g with f*g slightly >M, line search will check score; okay. Use 1+1e-8? If too loose, direction may increase. Use 1e-10.

`linprog` `time_limit` if very small maybe ignored? Use at least 0.5. But if phase slice small, maybe break before call if remaining<3s.

Could set `options={'presolve': 'highs', 'time_limit': tl, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-8, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-8}`. Keep simple.

In `_lp_refine`, if LP slow, time_limit should be less than slice. E.g., `lp_lim = min(4.0, max(0.6, stop - now - 1.0))`. For n<=192, 2s enough. For n=512, 5s maybe. Use `base_lim = 2.0 if n<=256 else 4.0`.

Iteration stopping: We need avoid too many stagnating with no improvement. Use max stag 40. But if nonmonotonic allowed, we can still accept current worse sometimes. Let's implement simple local current with ability to try worse perturbations but revert if no improvement.

Pseudo:
```
def _lp_refine(a0, stop, best, pool_add, rng, max_iter=None):
   a=_norm(a0); loc=_sc(a); _upd(a,best)
   if loc==inf: return a, loc
   stag=0; it=0; n=a.size
   # record local best to revert if wandering
   a_local=a.copy(); loc_local=loc
   while time.monotonic()<stop-2:
       it+=1
       if max_iter and it>max_iter: break
       lp_lim = min(4.0, 0.5 + (stop-now-2)*0.2) if stop-now>3 else 0.5
       g=_lp_dir(a, lp_lim)
       improved=False
       if g is not None:
           g=_norm(g)
           # maybe if g is almost same as a skip?
           if np.linalg.norm(g-a) > 1e-5:
               cand,sc=_blend(a,g,loc)
               if cand is not None:
                   a=cand; loc=sc; _upd(a,best); updated local; improved=True
           else:
               # no improvement via LP
               pass
       if not improved:
           # active local move occasionally
           if stag % 3 == 1:
              cand,sc=_local_peak(a, loc, rng)
              if cand is not None: update; improved=True
       if not improved:
           stag +=1
           # noise levels
           base = 0.05 if loc>1.52 else 0.02
           if rng.random() < 0.5:
               sig = min(0.15, base*(1+0.2*min(stag,20)))
               pa=_norm(a*np.exp(sig*rng.standard_normal(n)))
           else:
               pa=_norm(a + 0.01*a* rng.standard_normal(n))
           psa=_sc(pa)
           # Accept if better, or small worse for exploration if stag < 10
           if psa < loc*(1-1e-12):
               a=pa; loc=psa; _upd; stag=0; improved=True
           elif stag < 8 and psa < loc + 0.002 and rng.random()<0.2:
               a=pa; loc=psa; stag=0  # explore, don't update best
           # if current too worse than local best, revert
           if loc > loc_local + 0.01:
               a=a_local.copy(); loc=loc_local
       if improved:
           # update local best if loc < loc_local? Actually maintain current vs global
           if loc < loc_local - 1e-12:
               a_local=a.copy(); loc_local=loc
       if stag > 40:
           break
   # final local best if better than current? current may be worse; if a_local score better, update best
   _upd(a_local, best)
   _add_pool(a_local, loc_local)
   return a_local, loc_local
```
But if current explores worse and later improves, `_upd` called. If not, local best saved.

`_blend` should maybe try t=0.05 if g far. We can reduce evaluations by first evaluate t=1 and t=0.5; if t=1 good, extrapolate; else grid.
```
def _blend(f,g,loc):
    best_cand=None; best_s=loc
    tests=(1.0,0.5,0.9,0.7,0.3,0.2,0.75,0.4,0.1,0.05)
    # if g and f almost same: maybe return None
    for t in tests:
       cand=(1-t)*f + t*g
       cand=clip; norm
       s=_sc(cand)
       if s<best_s:
          best_s=s; best_cand=cand
          if s <= loc*(1-1e-8) and t==1.0: break? continue for extrapolate later
    if best_cand is None: return None,best_s
    # extrapolate from best_cand direction? If best_s from t=1, try g+alpha(g-f)
    if best_cand is g (t=1) or t_best==1:
       for alpha in (0.1,0.25,0.5):
          cand=g+alpha*(g-f); clip norm; s; update
    return best_cand,best_s
```
Need track t_best.

Potential performance: `_blend` normalizes each candidate; if f,g sum1 and convex, sum1; can skip normalization unless clipping/extrapolate. For convex, sum1. Just clip tiny negatives due round; maybe not. Use `cand = f + t*(g-f); if cand.min()<0: cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s>0 cand/=s else return None`.

`_local_peak`:
```
def _local_peak(a, loc, rng, top=3, tries=8):
   n=a.size; c=np.convolve(a,a)
   ks=np.argsort(c)[-top:]
   best=a; best_s=loc
   for _ in range(tries):
      k = int(rng.choice(ks))
      i = int(rng.integers(0,n))
      j = k-i
      if not (0<=j<n) or i==j: continue
      if i>j: i,j=j,i
      s=a[i]+a[j]
      if s<=0: continue
      for alpha in (0.0,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.7,0.8,0.9,0.95,1.0):
          a2=a.copy(); a2[i]=alpha*s; a2[j]=(1-alpha)*s
          a2=_norm(a2); if None continue
          sc=_sc(a2)
          if sc < best_s*(1-1e-12): best_s=sc; best=a2
   if best_s < loc: return best,best_s
   return None,loc
```
Setting alpha=0/1 zero one value; okay. But top indices from c maybe not central? Fine. Random pair each call; could be slow if 8*10 scores per iteration. Use only every few stag. Limit tries=5.

Maybe more effective: For active peak, choose i,j where product largest (i near middle for k) and split extreme. We can choose top pair by a_i*a_j. For each k, find pair with max product, split. Implement:

Now `_lse_polish` objective. Need top-level function? It can take `a0, stop, best, rng`.

Implement softmax efficient:
```
def _softmax(x):
   x=np.asarray(x,float)
   x=x-x.max()
   ex=np.exp(x)
   s=ex.sum()
   if s<=0: return None
   return ex/s
```
Bounds -40,40. In objective, if x outside due to minimize, clip. But L-BFGS respects bounds.

```
def _lse_polish(a0, stop, best, rng):
   a=_norm(a0); if None return
   n=a.size
   if n<8 or time.monotonic()>stop-10: return
   x=np.log(a+1e-12); x-=x.mean()
   bounds=[(-40.0,40.0)]*n
   # objective closure with beta nonlocal
   def objective(beta):
      def fun(x):
         x=np.clip(x, -40,40)
         a=np.exp(x); a/=a.sum()
         c=np.convolve(a,a)
         cm=float(c.max())
         vals=beta*(c-cm)
         # vals<=0, but due rounding >0 small; clip?
         vals=np.clip(vals, -50, 0.0) # safe
         z=np.exp(vals)
         zsum=z.sum()
         if zsum<=0: val=cm; w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
         else:
            val=cm + float(np.log(zsum))/beta
            w=z/zsum
         if not getattr(objective,'grad',False): return val
         # compute grad
         # D = sliding windows w dot a
         if sliding_window_view is not None and n <= 600:
             W=sliding_window_view(w, n)
             D = W @ a
         else:
             # loop
             D=np.empty(n)
             for j in range(n): D[j]=np.dot(a, w[j:j+n])
         grad_a=2.0*D
         ga_dot_a=float(grad_a.dot(a))
         gx=a*(grad_a - ga_dot_a)
         return val, gx
      return fun
   betas = [20,50,100,300,800,2000,5000,10000]
   for beta in betas:
      if time.monotonic()>stop-5: break
      try:
          min_iter=20; min_fun=60
          res=minimize(objective(beta), x, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':min_iter,'maxfun':min_fun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'disp':False,'maxcor':10})
          x_new=np.asarray(res.x,float)
          a2=_softmax(x_new)
          # fallback if failed
          if a2 is None: a2=a
          # Evaluate actual, maybe local peak? update best if improves
          old=best[0]
          s2=_sc(a2)
          if math.isfinite(s2) and s2<old:
              best[0]=s2; best[1]=a2.copy()
              a=a2.copy()
          x=np.log(a+1e-12); x-=x.mean()
      except Exception:
          break
```
The closure `objective` returns new function for each beta; not using `getattr`. Good.

But the gradient shift: `vals=np.clip(vals, -50, 0.0)` will make weights zero for too low peaks; okay. The value uses log zsum of clipped, still smooth-ish. For β large, only top peak(s). If all vals clipped to -50, zsum maybe underflow? Top vals include 0 -> exp(0)=1, so zsum≥1. Good.

Need ensure `sliding_window_view(w, n)` where w has length 2n-1 returns shape (n,n). This requires `n` positive. If n=1, shape (1,1). Good.

In gradient, if beta very large and weights one hot, grad_a=2 a shifted by peak. Good.

Possible performance: `W @ a` with W view may not use BLAS due non-contiguous; could be slow. We can do `W = sliding_window_view(w,n)`; then `D = np.asarray(W) @ a`? That copies. For n=192, fine. Use `np.ascontiguousarray(W)` maybe. But inside objective many calls; copy 0.3MB each call, okay. Let's do:
```

D = W.dot(a)  # may handle strides
```

`_norm`: global top-level? Define inside:
```
    def _norm(a):
       a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
       if a.size==0: return None
       a=np.nan_to_num(a,nan=0,posinf=0,neginf=0)
       a=np.clip(a,0,None)
       s=float(a.sum())
       if s < 1e-300 or not math.isfinite(s): return None
       return a/s
```

`_sc`:
```
    def _sc(a):
       a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
       if a.size==0: return inf
       a=np.nan_to_num(a,nan=0,posinf=0,neginf=0)
       a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
       s=float(a.sum())
       if s < 0.01 or not math.isfinite(s): return inf
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
       m=float(np.max(c))
       if not math.isfinite(m): return inf
       return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
Since normalized sum1, sum check 0.01 true.

`_lp_dir(f, tl)` as above. It uses `linprog` global. If n>900? Maybe matrix large; still okay. We can skip if `2*n-1 * n` too big memory? Set if n>1024 return None. Our n≤512. Use.

`_blend(f,g,loc)` nested uses `_sc`, `_norm`.
```
    def _blend(f,g,loc):
       if g is None or f is None: return None, loc
       if np.linalg.norm(f-g) < 1e-6: return None, loc
       best_c=None; best_s=loc; best_t=None
       for t in (1.0,0.5,0.9,0.75,0.35,0.65,0.25,0.15,0.7,0.4,0.1,0.85,0.05):
          cand=f + t*(g-f)
          if cand.min() < -1e-12:
             cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
          ssum=float(cand.sum())
          if ssum <= 1e-15: continue
          cand = cand/ssum
          sc=_sc(cand)
          if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
             best_s=sc; best_c=cand; best_t=t
             # if t=1 and sc very low, continue for extrapolation
       if best_c is None: return None, loc
       # extrapolate if best_t ==1 or best_t close
       if best_t is not None and best_t >= 0.95:
          for alpha in (0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0):
              cand = best_c + alpha*(best_c - f)  # since best_c approx g? If best_c from t=1 it's g.
              if cand.min() < 0: cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
              ssum=float(cand.sum()); if ssum<=1e-15: continue
              cand/=ssum
              sc=_sc(cand)
              if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                  best_s=sc; best_c=cand; best_t=None
       return best_c,best_s
```
If best_t not 1 but combination is better, extrapolating from that combo direction g-f at alpha may correspond t > best_t; fine.

Potential many scores: 13+4 per LP. Could be heavy but okay. Limit t list shorter: (1,0.75,0.5,0.35,0.2,0.1,0.9,0.6,0.4). Use 9. Good. To reduce, first evaluate g; if not much better, few.
```
order=(1.0,0.5,0.9,0.7,0.35,0.2,0.8,0.6,0.45,0.15)
```

`_local_peak(a, loc, rng)` nested. It evaluates candidates; call occasionally.
```
    def _local_peak(a, loc, rng, top=3, tries=10):
        n=a.size; c=np.convolve(a,a); ks=np.argsort(c)[-top:]
        best_c=a.copy(); best_s=loc
        rng_pairs=0
        for k in ks:
            lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
            # max product pair
            if hi-lo+1 >=2:
                ii=np.arange(lo,hi+1); jj=k-ii
                # distinct mask ii!=jj
                mask=(ii!=jj)
                if mask.any():
                    ii=ii[mask]; jj=jj[mask]
                    if ii.size:
                        idx=int(np.argmax(a[ii]*a[jj]))
                        i=int(ii[idx]); j=int(jj[idx]); s=a[i]+a[j]
                        for alpha in (0.0,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.65,0.8,0.9,1.0):
                            a2=a.copy(); a2[i]=alpha*s; a2[j]=(1-alpha)*s
                            cand=_norm(a2)
                            if cand is None: continue
                            sc=_sc(cand)
                            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                                best_s=sc; best_c=cand.copy()
                        # also random pairs from this k
                        for _ in range(3):
                            i=int(rng.integers(lo,hi+1)); j=k-i
                            if j<0 or j>=n or i==j: continue
                            if i>j: i,j=j,i
                            s=a[i]+a[j]
                            if s<=1e-15: continue
                            alpha=float(rng.choice([0.0,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.7,0.8,0.9,1.0]))
                            ...
        if best_s < loc - 1e-15: return best_c,best_s
        return None,loc
```
Maybe 3*10 +9 =39 convs; if called every 3 stag 13 per optimize, maybe heavy but okay for n=192. Limit tries top=2? Use top=2 and random 2 to reduce. We'll call only if stag>2 and not every iter? In `_lp_refine`, if not improved and stag>1 and stag%2==0. Good.

`_lp_refine` nested uses many. Let's code with current and local best.
```
    def _lp_refine(a0, stop, max_iter=100000):
        a=_norm(a0)
        if a is None: a=np.ones(192); ...
        loc=_sc(a)
        _update(a); if not finite: try ones
        n=a.size
        a_local=a.copy(); loc_local=loc
        stag=0; it=0
        while time.monotonic() < stop - 3.0:
            it+=1
            if it>max_iter: break
            now=time.monotonic()
            rem=stop-now
            if rem < 4.0: break
            g=None
            tl = min(4.0 if n<=300 else 6.0, max(0.6, rem-3.0))
            g=_lp_dir(a, tl)
            improved=False
            if g is not None:
                g=_norm(g)
                if g is not None and np.linalg.norm(g-a) > 1e-6:
                    cand,sc=_blend(a,g,loc)
                    if cand is not None:
                        a=cand; loc=sc; _update(a); improved=True
                        if loc < loc_local - 1e-15:
                            a_local=a.copy(); loc_local=loc
                        stag=0
            if not improved and stag >= 1 and (stag % 2 == 0):
                cand,sc=_local_peak(a, loc, rng)
                if cand is not None:
                    a=cand; loc=sc; _update(a); improved=True; stag=0
                    if loc < loc_local -1e-15: local
            if not improved:
                stag += 1
                # noise
                base = 0.04 if loc > 1.52 else 0.015
                sig = min(0.18, base * (1.0 + 0.25*min(stag, 30)))
                if rng.random() < 0.6:
                    pa = _norm(a * np.exp(sig * rng.standard_normal(n)))
                else:
                    pa = _norm(a * (1.0 + 0.5*sig*rng.standard_normal(n)))
                    # after normalized could have negative before norm? _norm clips
                if pa is not None:
                    psa=_sc(pa)
                    if math.isfinite(psa):
                        if psa < loc - 1e-12:
                            a=pa; loc=psa; _update(a); improved=True; stag=0
                            if loc < loc_local -1e-15: local
                        elif stag < 10 and psa < loc + 0.002 and rng.random() < 0.15:
                            a=pa; loc=psa; stag=0
                            # current worse than local; don't update local
            if loc > loc_local + 0.02 and stag > 3:
                a=a_local.copy(); loc=loc_local; stag=min(stag,5)
            if stag > 60: break
        # final update local
        _update(a_local)
        return a_local, loc_local
```
But if improved from LP but loc not lower than local (someday worse? cand only if sc<loc, but loc might already worse than local? We accept, local update only if lower than loc_local). If current worse than local due exploration and LP improves but still above local, local stays. Good.

In noise, if naive `a*(1+...)` can have negative, `_norm` clips; could produce very different. Fine.

If LP direction g is almost same (norm small) and no local, stag increments.

One concern: `_blend` uses `loc` as score of current a. But if current a is worse than `loc_local` due exploration, improvement over current may not improve local; `_update` only if global best. okay.

After `_lp_refine`, it returns local best, but if current had a global improvement not in local? `_update` updates best at improvement; local might be old if improvement was worse than local? That can't: improvement over current maybe global best but local best should be max of all local accepted? If current was worse and LP produced candidate better than current but still worse than local, global might not improve. If global improves, candidate score < previous global, which is ≤ local maybe if local was previous global? local is a sequence accepted locally; it may not be global best if global from another run? But for a single run, if candidate improves global, and local is our run's best, candidate likely < loc_local or local not updated because we only update local when loc<loc_local. We update local when improved and loc<loc_local. Good.

But when exploring worse, we don't call `_update`; if later candidate improves global, loc may still >local? impossible if local score is lower than candidate? If candidate improves global, global was best from previous local or other run. If local in this run is higher than global? possible if another run found better. Then candidate may improve global but still higher than local? Actually local ≥ global? local is a run's best, global min across runs; if global from other run lower than local, candidate could be between global and local? It improves global (score lower than global) thus lower than local (since local>global), local updates if condition loc<loc_local true. Good.

    def _lse_polish(a0, stop):
        a=_norm(a0)
        if a is None: return
        n=a.size
        if n<8: return
        x=np.log(a+1e-12); x-=x.mean()
        bounds=[(-40.0,40.0)]*n
        betas=[20,50,100,300,800,2000,5000,10000]
        for beta in betas:
            if time.monotonic()>stop-4: break
            def fun(x): ...
            try:
                res=minimize(fun, x, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':25, 'maxfun':80, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxcor':10, 'disp':False})
                x_new=np.asarray(res.x,float)
                a2=_softmax(x_new)
                if a2 is None: continue
                sc=_sc(a2)
                if math.isfinite(sc):
                    _update(a2)
                    if sc < best[0]+? maybe update a if improved or if local? To continue from best, set a if _update improved? If a2 not global best but maybe its smooth good? For next beta, start from a2 or best? Use a2 if finite even if not lower? Could hinder. Better if sc < best[0] or (sc < best[0]+0.01): a=a2.copy(); x=log...
                # If actual didn't improve but smooth descended, still might help later beta; but starting from a2 (worse actual) could be bad. Use best for next if not improved? Maybe use a2 if improved else keep old a.
                if math.isfinite(sc) and sc <= best[0] + 0.005:
                    a=a2.copy(); x=np.log(a+1e-12); x-=x.mean()
                # else keep x maybe res.x? Could produce bad. Keep best a.
            except Exception:
                break
```
Need `_softmax` inside:
```
        def _softmax(x):
            x=np.asarray(x,float).ravel()
            if x.size!=n: return None
            x=x-x.max()
            ex=np.exp(x); s=ex.sum()
            if s<=0 or not math.isfinite(s): return None
            return ex/s
```
Objective `fun`:
```
            def fun(x):
                x=np.clip(np.asarray(x,float), -40,40)
                ex=np.exp(x); a=ex/ex.sum()
                c=np.convolve(a,a)
                cm=float(c.max())
                vals=beta*(c-cm)
                vals=np.clip(vals, -50.0, 0.0)
                z=np.exp(vals)
                zsum=float(z.sum())
                if zsum <= 0: zsum=1.0; w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
                else: w=z/zsum
                val=cm + math.log(zsum)/beta
                # gradient
                if sliding_window_view is not None:
                    try:
                       W=sliding_window_view(w, n)
                       D=W.dot(a)
                    except Exception:
                       D=np.array([float(np.dot(a,w[j:j+n])) for j in range(n)])
                else: ...
                grad_a=2.0*D
                gx=a*(grad_a - float(grad_a.dot(a)))
                return val, gx
```
`sliding_window_view` with `w`, n: If w is non-contiguous? w from exp contiguous. Good.

But `fun` creates gradient for every objective call; L-BFGS with `jac=True` expects function returns tuple. Good.

Potential issue: For high beta, `vals` clipped at -50 makes many weights 0; gradient uses `W.dot(a)` where w sum 1. okay. `math.log(zsum)` if zsum <1? But at least one exp(0)=1 if any c==cm. Due c.max, at least one val=0. If multiple and `vals` maybe tiny negative? max vals=0. zsum≥1. Good.

What if `beta` small, vals range >50? Actually vals≤0, no overflow. Underflow for low peaks ok.

Gradient derivation with shifted val and clipping: If clip, gradient discontinuity; okay.

L-BFGS might take x outside bounds and fun clips gradient at bounds? It may not know; but bounds enforce. Clipping inside could cause gradient mismatch at bounds; bounds ±40, x likely inside. Good.

`_gen_starts` nested uses rng, par, best.
Need avoid infinite loop in dedup. Use list of out arrays.
```
    def _gen_starts(n, limit=12):
       if n<4: n=4
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       raw=[]
       def raw_add(a):
           a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
           if a.size!=n: return
           a=np.nan_to_num(a,nan=0,posinf=0,neginf=0)
           a=np.clip(a,0,None)
           if a.sum()<=1e-300: return
           raw.append(a)
       # interpolate par and best
       if par is not None and par.size>=2:
           ppar=np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,par.size?)) # Wait np.interp(xpos, xp, fp) where xp length par.size and fp par. xp must increasing: np.linspace(0,1,par.size)
           raw_add(ppar)
           # smoothed
           if n>=3:
              sm=np.convolve(ppar, np.array([0.25,0.5,0.25]), mode='same')
              raw_add(sm)
       if best[1].size>=2: similarly with best[1]
       ... shapes
       scored=[]
       out=[]; outvec=[]
       for a in raw:
          a=_norm(a)
          if a is None: continue
          # duplicate check
          dup=False
          for b in outvec:
             if np.linalg.norm(a-b) < 0.02: dup=True; break
          if dup: continue
          s=_sc(a)
          if math.isfinite(s):
             scored.append((s,a))
       scored.sort(key=lambda t:t[0])
       for s,a in scored[:limit]: out.append(a)
       return out if out else [np.ones(n)/n]
```
Need if `par.size` maybe 1; if n? handle.

Shape generators inside raw_add:
```
       raw_add(np.ones(n))
       for c in ... raw_add(np.exp(c*(x-0.5)))
       for p in ...:
          raw_add(x**p); raw_add((1-x)**p); raw_add((x*(1-x))**p)
       for k in range(1,6):
          for amp in (0.3,0.55,0.8):
             for ph in (0, math.pi/2, math.pi):
                raw_add(1+amp*np.sin(2*math.pi*k*x+ph))
                raw_add(1+amp*np.cos(2*math.pi*k*x+ph))
       for c in (0,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9,1.0):
          for w in (0.03,0.08,0.15,0.35,0.7):
             raw_add(np.exp(-0.5*((x-c)/w)**2))
       for c1,c2 in [(0,1),(0.25,0.75),(0.1,0.9)]:
          for w in (0.1,0.25,0.5):
             raw_add(np.exp(-((x-c1)/w)**2)+0.5*np.exp(-((x-c2)/w)**2))
       # random smooth 10
       for _ in range(12):
          m=int(rng.integers(3, max(4,n//8)))
          px=np.sort(rng.random(m)); vals=rng.lognormal(0,1,m)
          raw_add(np.interp(x, px, vals, left=vals[0], right=vals[-1]))
       for alpha in (0.1,0.3,0.7,1.5,3.0):
          try: raw_add(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
          except: pass
       # random binary smooth
       for _ in range(5):
          m=(rng.random(n)<0.5).astype(float)
          if m.sum()>0:
             if n>=3: raw_add(np.convolve(m, np.ones(3)/3, mode='same'))
             else: raw_add(m)
       # binomial
       for m in (2,5,12,32,100):
          if m < n-1: # trunc? full binomial length m+1 padded with zeros? We can load
             vals=np.empty(n); 
             # generate normalized safely using log? 
             # For m up to 100, np.binomial ok? maybe overflow for m=300. Use recurrence.
             v=1.0
             for i in range(min(m+1,n)):
                 vals[i]=v
                 v *= (m-i)/(i+1) if i<m else 0
             if n>m+1: vals[m+1:]=0
             raw_add(vals)
       # reversed binomial not needed
```

Phase functions:
```
    def _run_phase(n, stop):
       if n<4: return
       starts=_gen_starts(n, limit=12)
       ran=[]
       for arr in starts:
          now=time.monotonic()
          if now >= stop - 8: break
          # skip if similar to ran
          if any(np.linalg.norm(arr-r)<0.02 for r in ran): continue
          ran.append(arr)
          slice_stop = now + min(50.0, max(8.0, (stop-now-8)/max(1,len(starts)-len(ran)+1)))
          if slice_stop > stop-8: slice_stop = stop-8
          _lp_refine(arr, slice_stop)
```

    n_par = par.size if par is not None else 192

    maxn=512

    # combine fractions for same n, filter 32<=n<=600? If n_par > maxn use it with fraction maybe 0.5 but limit <=768.
    frac_map={}
    for n,fr in phase_specs:

       if n>768: continue

       frac_map[n]=frac_map.get(n,0)+fr
    total=fr_sum
    # sort: parent first, then by fraction desc
    order=sorted(frac_map.items(), key=lambda kv: (kv[0]!=n_par, -kv[1]))
    remaining_work = max(10.0, deadline - t0)
    for i,(n,fr) in enumerate(order):
       phase_time = remaining_work * fr / total
       if phase_time < 5: continue
       stop_phase = time.monotonic() + phase_time
       if stop_phase > deadline: stop_phase = deadline
       _run_phase(n, stop_phase)
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
```

    def _mix(stop):
        if best[1].size < 2: return
        n=best[1].size
        # Use _gen_starts with limit=8; includes parent and best and random

        # add pool candidates interpolated to n

        for ps,pa in pool:
            if pa is None: continue
            if pa.size != n:
                arr=np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,pa.size), pa)

           if now >= stop-3: break
           if _similar(arr, best[1], 0.01): continue
           # blend

           if math.isfinite(sarr) and sarr < best[0]+0.05:
               slice_stop=min(stop-2, now+10)
               _lp_refine(_norm(arr), slice_stop)
```

`W.dot(a)` where W is sliding window view of w. Let's verify shape: `w` length 2n-1. `sliding_window_view(w, n)` returns shape `(2n-1 - n + 1, n) = (n, n)`. Row j = w[j:j+n]. Correct. `D = W.dot(a)` gives each row dot a: ∑_m w[j+m] a[m]. Good.

Now, objective gradient for LSE: For each peak k, derivative c_k = ∑_{i} a_i a_{k-i}. We use D[j]=∑_m a_m w_{j+m} where m=k-j, so k=j+m. This derivative included both ordered pairs? Let's re-evaluate. c_k = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_i a_{k-i}, where term exists if k-i in [0,n-1]. This ordered sum includes both (i,j) and (j,i) when i≠j because as i runs all, j runs all. Derivative wrt a_j: Consider terms where first index i=j: a_j a_{k-j}. Terms where second index k-i=j => i=k-j: a_{k-j} a_j. If j≠k-j, these are two distinct terms in sum, each derivative a_{k-j}; total 2a_{k-j}. If j=k/2, one term a_j^2 derivative 2a_j. So derivative 2 a_{k-j}. Thus `grad_a_j = 2 ∑_k w_k a_{k-j}`. Our D[j] = ∑_m a_m w_{j+m}, with m=k-j => k=j+m, yes. Good.

But our c arrays from np.convolve are ordered sum? `np.convolve(a,a)[k] = ∑_{i} a_i a_{k-i}`. Yes. Good.

Now, in `_local_peak`, we choose pair i,j with i+j=k and reduce product. But c_k ordered sum contains two products for distinct pair; setting alpha affects both. Good.

Potential alternative: The LP constraint A*g ≤1 uses A row k with f_{k-i}; same ordered. Good.

Now, consider if in LP, building A with column j: rows j..j+n-1 = fM. That gives A[k,j]=fM[k-j]. Yes.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None

try:
    from numpy.lib.stride_tricks import sliding_window_view
except Exception:
    sliding_window_view = None


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    try:
        B = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        B = 1300.0

    # Leave hard headroom for final validation/return.
    hard = t0 + max(30.0, B - 30.0)
    work_deadline = t0 + max(30.0, B - 150.0) if B > 300 else max(t0 + 30.0, hard - 20.0)
    late_deadline = min(work_deadline, hard - 120.0) if B > 300 else hard - 20.0

    best = [float("inf"), np.ones(2, dtype=np.float64)]
    pool = []

    # Read the supplied parent construction, if present.
    par = None
    try:
        _h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if _h is not None:
            par = np.asarray(_h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            par = np.nan_to_num(par, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            par = np.clip(par, 0.0, None)
            if par.size == 0 or not math.isfinite(float(par.sum())) or float(par.sum()) <= 1e-300:
                par = None
    except Exception:
        par = None

    def _norm(a):
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return None
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            return None
        return a / s

    def _sc(a):
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return float("inf")
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
        if not math.isfinite(m):
            return float("inf")
        return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))

    def _small_dist(a, b, th=0.02):
        a = _norm(a)
        b = _norm(b)
        if a is None or b is None or a.size != b.size:
            return False
        return float(np.linalg.norm(a - b)) < th

    def _add_pool(a, score):
        if score is None or not math.isfinite(score):
            return
        a = _norm(a)
        if a is None:
            return
        if score > best[0] + 0.25:
            return
        for idx, (ps, pa) in enumerate(pool):
            if pa.size == a.size and float(np.linalg.norm(a - pa)) < 0.02:
                if score < ps - 1e-12:
                    pool[idx] = (float(score), a.copy())
                return
        pool.append((float(score), a.copy()))
        pool.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
        if len(pool) > 60:
            pool.pop()

    def _update(a):
        a = _norm(a)
        if a is None:
            return
        s = _sc(a)
        if math.isfinite(s) and s < best[0]:
            best[0] = float(s)
            best[1] = a.copy()
            _add_pool(a, s)

    # Initial best candidate.
    if par is not None:
        _update(par)
    _update(np.ones(192, dtype=np.float64) / 192.0)
    if not math.isfinite(best[0]):
        best[0] = 2.0
        best[1] = np.ones(192, dtype=np.float64) / 192.0

    def _lp_dir(f, time_limit):
        if linprog is None:
            return None
        f = _norm(f)
        if f is None:
            return None
        n = f.size
        if n < 2 or n > 1024:
            return None
        c = np.convolve(f, f)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
        if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
            return None
        ff = f / M
        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = ff
        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, 1.0 + 1e-10, dtype=np.float64)
        obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        try:
            options = {"presolve": True, "time_limit": max(0.6, float(time_limit))}
            r = linprog(
                obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options=options,
            )
        except Exception:
            return None
        x = getattr(r, "x", None)
        if x is None:
            return None
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if x.size != n:
            return None
        x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
        s = float(x.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            return None
        return x / s

    def _blend(f, g, loc):
        f = _norm(f)
        g = _norm(g)
        if f is None or g is None:
            return None, loc
        if f.size == 0 or f.size != g.size:
            return None, loc
        if float(np.linalg.norm(f - g)) < 1e-7:
            return None, loc

        best_c = None
        best_s = float(loc)
        best_t = None

        tvals = (1.0, 0.5, 0.9, 0.7, 0.35, 0.2, 0.8, 0.6, 0.45, 0.25, 0.1, 0.95)
        for t in tvals:
            cand = f + t * (g - f)
            if cand.min() < 0.0:
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            sm = float(cand.sum())
            if sm <= 1e-300:
                continue
            cand = cand / sm
            sc = _sc(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                best_s = sc
                best_c = cand
                best_t = t

        if best_c is None:
            return None, loc

        # Extrapolate past the best convex point in the same direction.
        for alpha in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0):
            cand = best_c + alpha * (best_c - f)
            if cand.min() < 0.0:
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            sm = float(cand.sum())
            if sm <= 1e-300:
                continue
            cand = cand / sm
            sc = _sc(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                best_s = sc
                best_c = cand
                best_t = None

        return best_c, best_s

    def _local_peak(a, loc, rng):
        a = _norm(a)
        if a is None:
            return None, loc
        n = a.size
        if n < 2:
            return None, loc
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        if c.size == 0:
            return None, loc
        top = min(3, n)
        ks = np.argsort(c)[-top:]

        best_c = a.copy()
        best_s = float(loc)

        alphas = (0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.65, 0.8, 0.9, 1.0)

        for k in ks:
            k = int(k)
            lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
            hi = min(n - 1, k)
            if hi < lo:
                continue

            ii = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            jj = k - ii
            mask = ii != jj
            if mask.any():
                ii2 = ii[mask]
                jj2 = jj[mask]
                if ii2.size:
                    prod = a[ii2] * a[jj2]
                    idx = int(np.argmax(prod))
                    i = int(ii2[idx])
                    j = int(jj2[idx])
                    s = a[i] + a[j]
                    if s > 1e-300:
                        for al in alphas:
                            a2 = a.copy()
                            a2[i] = al * s
                            a2[j] = (1.0 - al) * s
                            cand = _norm(a2)
                            if cand is None:
                                continue
                            sc = _sc(cand)
                            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                                best_s = sc
                                best_c = cand.copy()

            for _ in range(2):
                i = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
                j = k - i
                if not (0 <= j < n) or i == j:
                    continue
                if i > j:
                    i, j = j, i
                s = a[i] + a[j]
                if s <= 1e-300:
                    continue
                al = float(alpha for alpha in alphas if rng.random() < 1.0)
                al = float(rng.choice(alphas))
                a2 = a.copy()
                a2[i] = al * s
                a2[j] = (1.0 - al) * s
                cand = _norm(a2)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                sc = _sc(cand)
                if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-15:
                    best_s = sc
                    best_c = cand.copy()

        if best_s < float(loc) - 1e-15:
            return best_c, best_s
        return None, loc

    def _lp_refine(a0, stop, max_iter=1000000):
        a = _norm(a0)
        if a is None:
            try:
                n0 = int(np.size(a0))
            except Exception:
                n0 = 192
            if n0 < 4:
                n0 = 192
            a = np.ones(n0, dtype=np.float64) / float(n0)

        loc = _sc(a)
        _update(a)
        if not math.isfinite(loc):
            return a, loc

        n = a.size
        a_local = a.copy()
        loc_local = loc
        stag = 0
        it = 0

        while True:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= stop - 3.0:
                break
            it += 1
            if it > max_iter:
                break
            rem = stop - now
            if rem < 5.0:
                break

            improved = False

            tl = min(5.0 if n <= 320 else 8.0, max(0.7, rem - 4.0))
            if tl >= 0.7:
                g = _lp_dir(a, tl)
                if g is not None:
                    g = _norm(g)
                    if g is not None and float(np.linalg.norm(g - a)) > 1e-6:
                        cand, sc = _blend(a, g, loc)
                        if cand is not None and math.isfinite(sc):
                            a = cand
                            loc = sc
                            _update(a)
                            improved = True
                            stag = 0
                            if loc < loc_local - 1e-15:
                                a_local = a.copy()
                                loc_local = loc

            if not improved and stag >= 3 and stag % 3 == 0:
                cand, sc = _local_peak(a, loc, rng)
                if cand is not None and math.isfinite(sc):
                    a = cand
                    loc = sc
                    _update(a)
                    improved = True
                    stag = 0
                    if loc < loc_local - 1e-15:
                        a_local = a.copy()
                        loc_local = loc

            if not improved:
                stag += 1
                base = 0.045 if loc > 1.52 else 0.018
                sig = min(0.20, base * (1.0 + 0.25 * min(stag, 30)))
                if rng.random() < 0.65:
                    pa = _norm(a * np.exp(sig * rng.standard_normal(n)))
                else:
                    pa = _norm(a * (1.0 + 0.45 * sig * rng.standard_normal(n)))

                if pa is not None:
                    psa = _sc(pa)
                    if math.isfinite(psa):
                        if psa < loc - 1e-12:
                            a = pa
                            loc = psa
                            _update(a)
                            improved = True
                            stag = 0
                            if loc < loc_local - 1e-15:
                                a_local = a.copy()
                                loc_local = loc
                        elif stag < 12 and psa < loc + 0.003 and rng.random() < 0.15:
                            # Controlled exploration, not necessarily a global improvement.
                            a = pa
                            loc = psa
                            stag = 0

            if loc > loc_local + 0.02 and stag > 3:
                a = a_local.copy()
                loc = loc_local
                stag = min(stag, 3)

            if stag > 70:
                break

        _update(a_local)
        _add_pool(a_local, loc_local)
        return a_local, loc_local

    def _softmax(x):
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if x.size == 0:
            return None
        x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=-40.0, posinf=40.0, neginf=-40.0)
        x = np.clip(x, -40.0, 40.0)
        x = x - float(x.max())
        ex = np.exp(x)
        s = float(ex.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            return None
        return ex / s

    def _lse_polish(a0, stop):
        if minimize is None:
            return
        a = _norm(a0)
        if a is None:
            return
        n = a.size
        if n < 8:
            return

        x = np.log(a + 1e-12)
        x = x - float(x.mean())
        bounds = [(-40.0, 40.0)] * n
        betas = (20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 300.0, 800.0, 2000.0, 5000.0, 10000.0)

        for beta in betas:
            if time.monotonic() > stop - 4.0:
                break

            def fun(x):
                x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
                if x.size != n:
                    return float("inf"), np.zeros(n)
                x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=-40.0, posinf=40.0, neginf=-40.0)
                x = np.clip(x, -40.0, 40.0)
                x = x - float(x.max())
                ex = np.exp(x)
                a = ex / float(ex.sum())

                c = np.convolve(a, a)
                cm = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
                vals = beta * (c - cm)
                vals = np.clip(vals, -50.0, 0.0)
                z = np.exp(vals)
                zsum = float(z.sum())
                if not math.isfinite(zsum) or zsum <= 1e-300:
                    w = np.zeros_like(c)
                    if c.size:
                        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
                    zsum = 1.0
                else:
                    w = z / zsum
                val = cm + math.log(zsum) / beta

                if sliding_window_view is not None:
                    try:
                        W = sliding_window_view(w, n)
                        D = W.dot(a)
                    except Exception:
                        D = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
                        for j in range(n):
                            D[j] = float(np.dot(a, w[j:j + n]))
                else:
                    D = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    for j in range(n):
                        D[j] = float(np.dot(a, w[j:j + n]))

                grad_a = 2.0 * D
                gx = a * (grad_a - float(np.dot(grad_a, a)))
                return val, gx

            try:
                res = minimize(
                    fun,
                    x,
                    jac=True,
                    method="L-BFGS-B",
                    bounds=bounds,
                    options={
                        "maxiter": 25,
                        "maxfun": 90,
                        "ftol": 1e-12,
                        "gtol": 1e-8,
                        "maxcor": 10,
                        "disp": False,
                    },
                )
                x_new = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel() if hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None else None
                if x_new is not None:
                    a2 = _softmax(x_new)
                    if a2 is not None:
                        sc = _sc(a2)
                        if math.isfinite(sc):
                            _update(a2)
                            # Continue from a nearby good point, not a far worse one.
                            if sc <= best[0] + 0.012:
                                a = a2.copy()
                                x = np.log(a + 1e-12)
                                x = x - float(x.mean())
            except Exception:
                break

    def _gen_starts(n, limit=12):
        n = int(n)
        if n < 4:
            n = 4
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        raw = []

        def raw_add(a):
            a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if a.size != n:
                return
            a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
            if a.size == 0 or float(a.sum()) <= 1e-300:
                return
            raw.append(a)

        # Parent construction, if available.
        if par is not None and par.size >= 2:
            px = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, par.size)
            pbuf = np.interp(x, px, par, left=0.0, right=0.0)
            raw_add(pbuf)
            if n >= 3:
                raw_add(np.convolve(pbuf, np.array([0.25, 0.5, 0.25], dtype=np.float64), mode="same"))

        # Current best, interpolated to this length.
        bb = _norm(best[1])
        if bb is not None and bb.size >= 2:
            bx = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, bb.size)
            raw_add(np.interp(x, bx, bb, left=0.0, right=0.0))

        # Simple deterministic families.
        raw_add(np.ones(n))
        for c in (-3.0, -2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
            raw_add(np.exp(c * (x - 0.5)))
        for p in (0.2, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
            raw_add(x ** p)
            raw_add((1.0 - x) ** p)
            raw_add((x * (1.0 - x)) ** p)

        for m in range(1, 7):
            for amp in (0.30, 0.55, 0.80):
                for ph in (0.0, math.pi / 2.0, math.pi):
                    raw_add(1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * m * x + ph))
                    raw_add(1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * m * x + ph))

        for c in (0.0, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 0.90, 1.00):
            for w in (0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.35, 0.70):
                raw_add(np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - c) / w) ** 2))

        for c1, c2 in ((0.0, 1.0), (0.25, 0.75), (0.10, 0.90)):
            for w in (0.10, 0.25, 0.50):
                raw_add(np.exp(-((x - c1) / w) ** 2) + 0.5 * np.exp(-((x - c2) / w) ** 2))

        # Binomial-like shapes.
        for m in (2, 5, 12, 32, 100):
            vals = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v = 1.0
            for i in range(min(m + 1, n)):
                vals[i] = v
                if i < m:
                    v *= float(m - i) / float(i + 1)
            if np.max(vals) > 0:
                raw_add(vals)

        # Random smooth positives.
        for _ in range(12):
            m = int(rng.integers(3, max(4, n // 8)))
            px = np.sort(rng.random(m))
            pv = rng.lognormal(0.0, 1.0, m)
            raw_add(np.interp(x, px, pv, left=pv[0], right=pv[-1]))

        # Random Dirichlet shapes.
        for alpha in (0.1, 0.3, 0.7, 1.5, 3.0):
            try:
                raw_add(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
            except Exception:
                pass

        # Random binary masks, slightly smoothed.
        for _ in range(5):
            m = (rng.random(n) < 0.5).astype(np.float64)
            if float(m.sum()) > 0:
                if n >= 3:
                    raw_add(np.convolve(m, np.ones(3, dtype=np.float64) / 3.0, mode="same"))
                else:
                    raw_add(m)

        candidates = []
        scored = []
        for a in raw:
            an = _norm(a)
            if an is None:
                continue
            dup = False
            for b in candidates:
                if float(np.linalg.norm(an - b)) < 0.02:
                    dup = True
                    break
            if dup:
                continue
            s = _sc(an)
            if math.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, an))
                candidates.append(an)

        scored.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
        out = [a for _, a in scored[:max(1, int(limit))]]
        if not out:
            out = [np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)]
        return out

    def _run_phase(n, stop):
        n = int(n)
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= stop - 5.0:
            return
        starts = _gen_starts(n, limit=14)
        used = []
        for arr in starts:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= stop - 5.0:
                break
            skip = False
            for u in used:
                if _small_dist(arr, u, 0.02):
                    skip = True
                    break
            if skip:
                continue
            used.append(arr)
            remaining_slots = max(1, len(starts) - len(used) + 1)
            alloc = min(55.0, max(6.0, (stop - now - 5.0) / float(remaining_slots)))
            slice_stop = min(stop - 3.0, now + alloc)
            _lp_refine(arr, slice_stop)
            if time.monotonic() >= stop - 4.0:
                break

    def _mix_phase(stop):
        if best[1] is None or best[1].size < 2:
            return
        n = int(best[1].size)
        cands = _gen_starts(n, limit=10)

        for ps, pa in pool:
            if pa.size < 2:
                continue
            if pa.size == n:
                arr = pa
            else:
                arr = np.interp(
                    np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n),
                    np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, pa.size),
                    pa,
                    left=0.0,
                    right=0.0,
                )
            cands.append(arr)
            if len(cands) >= 18:
                break

        best_arr = _norm(best[1])
        if best_arr is None:
            return

        for arr in cands:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= stop - 3.0:
                break
            ar = _norm(arr)
            if ar is None or ar.size != best_arr.size:
                continue
            if _small_dist(ar, best_arr, 0.01):
                continue
            cand, sc = _blend(best_arr, ar, best[0])
            if cand is not None and math.isfinite(sc):
                _update(cand)
            sarc = _sc(ar)
            if math.isfinite(sarc) and sarc < best[0] + 0.06:
                slice_stop = min(stop - 2.0, now + 12.0)
                _lp_refine(ar, slice_stop)

    try:
        # If no strong parent, do a quick multi-start seed generation.
        if par is None or best[0] > 1.56:
            nq = int(par.size) if par is not None else 192
            for arr in _gen_starts(nq, limit=10):
                _update(arr)

        n_par = int(par.size) if par is not None else 192
        n_par = max(32, min(768, n_par))

        phase_defs = [
            (n_par, 0.55),
            (256, 0.12),
            (n_par, 0.08),
            (320, 0.08),
            (384, 0.07),
            (512, 0.05),
            (640, 0.05),
        ]

        frac_map = {}
        for nn, fr in phase_defs:
            if 32 <= int(nn) <= 768:
                frac_map[int(nn)] = frac_map.get(int(nn), 0.0) + float(fr)

        totalfr = sum(frac_map.values())
        if totalfr <= 0:
            frac_map = {n_par: 1.0}
            totalfr = 1.0

        order = sorted(frac_map.items(), key=lambda kv: (kv[0] != n_par, -kv[1]))
        rem_work = max(5.0, late_deadline - time.monotonic())

        for nn, fr in order:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= late_deadline - 5.0:
                break
            alloc = rem_work * float(fr) / totalfr
            if alloc < 8.0:
                continue
            stop_phase = min(late_deadline, now + alloc)
            _run_phase(int(nn), stop_phase)
            if time.monotonic() >= late_deadline:
                break

        # Use any leftover working time by re-refining the current best.
        while time.monotonic() < late_deadline - 8.0:
            slice_stop = min(late_deadline - 2.0, time.monotonic() + 45.0)
            if slice_stop - time.monotonic() < 5.0:
                break
            _lp_refine(_norm(best[1]), slice_stop)

        # Mixing / interpolation phase.
        if time.monotonic() < hard - 100.0:
            mix_stop = min(hard - 80.0, time.monotonic() + 60.0)
            _mix_phase(mix_stop)

        # Smooth log-sum-exp polish.
        if time.monotonic() < hard - 50.0:
            polish_stop = min(hard - 30.0, time.monotonic() + max(20.0, (hard - 30.0) - time.monotonic()))
            _lse_polish(_norm(best[1]), polish_stop)

        # Final LP polish until there is not much headroom.
        final_stop = hard - 6.0
        while time.monotonic() < final_stop - 8.0:
            slice_stop = min(final_stop - 2.0, time.monotonic() + 30.0)
            if slice_stop - time.monotonic() < 4.0:
                break
            _lp_refine(_norm(best[1]), slice_stop)

    except Exception:
        # Preserve whatever valid best candidate has been found so far.
        pass

    # Final validation and conversion to the required Python list.
    fin = _norm(best[1])
    if fin is None:
        fin = np.ones(16, dtype=np.float64)

    fin = np.nan_to_num(fin, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    fin = np.clip(fin, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(fin.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        if s > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s):
            fin = fin * (1.0 / s)
        else:
            fin = np.ones(16, dtype=np.float64)

    try:
        v = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in fin])
    except Exception:
        v = _sc(fin)

    if not math.isfinite(v):
        fin = np.ones(16, dtype=np.float64)
        try:
            v = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in fin])
        except Exception:
            v = _sc(fin)

    try:
        return [float(x) for x in fin]
    except Exception:
        return [1.0]
```
